Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm explaining this right, but... I guess I'm here because I've been having a little trouble with my daily routine. It's probably nothing too serious, I just... well, I've been losing a lot of time—sometimes hours, actually—just rechecking things. 

It's mostly just everyday stuff. Like my door locks, or things for my engineering work, like file dimensions and revision notes... even just my dressing supplies in the morning. I just get this feeling that I'm not, um, allowed to move on to the next task until I've gone through the exact same routine enough times. I basically have to keep doing it until the anxiety finally drops. I know that probably sounds really irrational, and I'm sorry if it's a bit strange. Does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you usually help people with?

The routines just sort of... eat up all the time I used to keep for myself. I feel really guilty about letting my friends down when I don't show up.

It's probably nothing too serious, I just... well, I've been losing a lot of time—sometimes hours, actually—just rechecking things. \n\nIt's mostly just everyday stuff. Like my door locks, or things for my engineering work, like file dimensions and revision notes... even just my dressing supplies in the morning. I just get this feeling that I'm not, um, allowed to move on to the next task until I've gone through the exact same routine enough times. I basically have to keep doing it until the anxiety finally drops. I know that probably sounds really irrational, and I'm sorry if it's a bit strange. Does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you usually help people with?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these routines or checks, or is it something you can stop doing if you want to?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'd really like to say that I can just stop whenever I want. I try to, I really do. I'll tell myself, 'Okay, you looked at the revision notes, you checked the locks, it's fine.' But... if I try to just walk away or skip the rest of the routine, the anxiety just spikes. \n\nIt's like I was mentioning before, I just don't feel *allowed* to move on to the next thing. I basically have to stay there and keep repeating the checks until that awful feeling finally drops. That's how I end up losing those hours I mentioned. I really wish I could just snap out of it and be normal about it, and I'm sorry if that makes me sound completely unhinged. Am I... am I explaining that clearly enough for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to think specific thoughts or repeat certain words or phrases in your head as part of these routines?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I really do that. I don't repeat specific words or phrases in my head, at least not that I've noticed. I hope that's an okay answer, I don't want to be unhelpful. \n\nIt's really just... well, I know I'm sort of repeating myself, and I'm really sorry to sound like a broken record, but it's mostly just the physical act of checking. Like I mentioned before, it's just going through the actual routine with the door locks or the file dimensions at work. I don't really have a specific thought in my head other than just waiting for that anxiety to drop so I feel allowed to move on to the next thing. \n\nIs that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Are these rituals and repeated checks affecting your relationships or work performance?\n\nPatient: Well... I try really hard not to let it inconvenience anyone else. I really want to be a good employee, you know? But, um... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, my project deadlines have started slipping a little bit. \n\nIt's not that I'm slacking off, I promise. I just... I end up staying late at the office, just trying to get comfortable enough with everything to actually submit the work. I hope that doesn't make me sound incompetent. I really do care about my job. \n\nAnd as for outside of work... well, I used to go to these paddleboarding meetups, and I liked to work out, but I've kind of stopped making it to those. The routines just sort of... eat up all the time I used to keep for myself. I feel really guilty about letting my friends down when I don't show up. \n\nI hope I'm not sounding like a complete mess. I'm really trying my best to keep it all together. Is it... is it normal for things to spill over into my schedule like that? Am I giving you the kind of information you need?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any intrusive or unwanted thoughts that disturb you or cause you distress?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess... I mean, I really don't want to sound like I'm completely losing my grip or anything. But... yeah, I do get these thoughts sometimes that just won't leave me alone. \n\nLike, even after I *know* I’ve cleaned a wound area perfectly well, or... or at work, after I've reviewed a CAD file over and over. I'll just get this thought cutting in that I missed one tiny little thing. And my brain just insists that this one tiny oversight is going to turn into a horrible infection, or... or a design mistake that actually hurts someone. \n\nI try so hard to just reason it away and tell myself it's fine, but I just can't. It just sticks there until I do something about it. I know that probably sounds incredibly irrational, and I'm really sorry if it's weird... is that the kind of thing you meant? I just hope I'm not sounding too crazy to you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I'll just get this thought cutting in that I missed one tiny little thing. And my brain just insists that this one tiny oversight is going to turn into a horrible infection, or... or a design mistake that actually hurts someone. \n\nI try so hard to just reason it away and tell myself it's fine, but I just can't. It just sticks there until I do something about it. I know that probably sounds incredibly irrational, and I'm really sorry if it's weird... is that the kind of thing you meant? I just hope I'm not sounding too crazy to you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these intrusive thoughts are coming from your own mind or do they feel like they're imposed on you from the outside?\n\nPatient: Um, no, they definitely feel like they're coming from me. I'm definitely not trying to think these things, it's just... my own thoughts getting kind of stuck, I guess? \n\nI just want to be clear that I know it's all in my own head. Does that make sense? I hope that's a normal way to experience it.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these intrusive thoughts cause you?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I mean, it's not like I'm completely falling apart or anything. I really don't want you to think I'm incapable of handling my life, or that I'm just sitting around crying. I try really hard to stay positive and just push through it! \n\nBut, I guess... if I'm being totally honest, it is pretty frustrating. It just makes me feel really tense and sort of... trapped, I guess? At least until I can go back and re-check the file or, you know, do whatever my brain is telling me to do. \n\nIt's not unbearable, I promise. I manage it okay most days. It's just really uncomfortable when it gets stuck in my head like that. \n\nI hope I'm not making a bigger deal out of this than I should be. I know other people probably deal with way worse things. Does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're checking things to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess that's a big part of it. I mean, I really try not to let it take over, and I don't want to sound like I'm completely obsessed or anything... but, well, the truth is, I do end up spending a lot of time checking things. \n\nLike, I'll check my dressing supplies to make sure they're perfectly clean, or I'll check the door locks, or at work, I'll go over the file dimensions and revision notes again and again. So I just take a little extra time. 

And, um, outside of work... I guess I haven't been going to my paddleboarding meetups or my regular workouts as much recently. The checking stuff just kind of eats up the time I used to keep for myself. But I'm sure it's just a phase, and I'll get back on track soon! 

I really hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible employee or anything. I'm still working really hard! Is it okay that I'm struggling with this a little bit? I just want to be honest with you so you can help me.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... maybe a little bit? I mean, I don't want to sound like I think the world is going to end or anything crazy like that. I know realistically that checking a lock won't stop everything bad in the world. 

But... I guess, yeah, I do kind of feel like I have to do it to keep things safe. It's actually sort of the main reason I decided to come see you. I'm just... I'm losing hours every day to rechecking things. Like, um, my dressing supplies in the morning, the door locks before I leave, and then at work it's the file dimensions and my revision notes. 

I just... I don't feel allowed to move on to the next thing until I've gone through the whole routine enough times. It's like my anxiety just stays so high until I do it just right, and then it finally drops and I can breathe again. I just want to make sure everything is okay and no one gets hurt or messed up because of me. 

I really hope that doesn't sound too strange or obsessive. I just want to be a responsible person, you know? Is it normal to feel like you have to do things a certain way to make the anxiety go away? I hope I'm explaining this right.

I'm definitely not trying to think these things, it's just... my own thoughts getting kind of stuck, I guess? \n\nI just want to be clear that I know it's all in my own head. Does that make sense? I hope that's a normal way to experience it.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these intrusive thoughts cause you?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I mean, it's not like I'm completely falling apart or anything. I really don't want you to think I'm incapable of handling my life, or that I'm just sitting around crying. I try really hard to stay positive and just push through it! \n\nBut, I guess... if I'm being totally honest, it is pretty frustrating. It just makes me feel really tense and sort of... trapped, I guess? At least until I can go back and re-check the file or, you know, do whatever my brain is telling me to do. \n\nIt's not unbearable, I promise. I manage it okay most days. It's just really uncomfortable when it gets stuck in my head like that. \n\nI hope I'm not making a bigger deal out of this than I should be. I know other people probably deal with way worse things. Um, well... I really try my best not to let it get in the way of things. I always want to be a good employee and, you know, stay on top of everything. But... I guess if I'm being completely honest, maybe it has impacted things a little bit?
